Its grid cells become polygons; a wide entrance gets transitions every `spacing` in place of the paper's two, and an entrance never joins two connected parts of a cluster, so no way across goes unused. The end smoothing is the funnel of the package. |
| Debug draw (`navmesh/debug`) | A Margo design over `@margoengine/lines`; no published source is needed for drawing edges. |
| Voxel navmesh (`navmesh/voxel`) | A Margo design: block columns stand in for the solid spans step 1 of `./build` would make, and the rest of that pipeline runs unchanged. No outside source. |
| Flying navigation (`navmesh/volume`) | D. Brewer, "3D Flight Navigation Using Sparse Voxel Octrees", Game AI Pro 3, chapter 21, 2017 (read in full): Morton order, 4 x 4 x 4 leaves in 64 bits (here a pair of u32, so no BigInt), bottom-up build, childless nodes as free space. Margo walks the tree to find the cells across a face and does not store the chapter's neighbour links. A. Nash, S. Koenig, C. Tovey, "Lazy Theta*: Any-Angle Path Planning and Path Length Analysis in 3D", AAAI 2010 (read in full): Algorithm 3, one sight check per expanded cell, straight distance as the estimate. |

## Entities and components: `@margoengine/ecs`

The store is a structure of arrays, as the package README describes. The batch 167 timing drives bitECS 0.4.0 as a black box from its public introduction (https://github.com/NateTheGreatt/bitECS/blob/master/docs/Intro.md): one query, position plus velocity, 100,000 entities. The library source was not opened.

## State machines: `@margoengine/fsm`

Written clean room from the published work below. No source of a statechart library or another engine was read. Sismic is run as an outside tool through its documented interface (`packages/fsm/test/outside/`); its source was not read. The batch 167 timing drives XState 5.33.4 as a black box from its package README (https://github.com/statelyai/xstate/tree/main/packages/core#readme): 10,000 transitions of a two-state toggle. The library source was not opened.

| Part | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Hierarchy, parallel regions, `in(S)` guards (`fsm`, `fsm/parallel`) | D. Harel, "Statecharts: a visual formalism for complex systems", Science of Computer Programming 8(3), 1987 |
| Choosing before moving, inner transitions before outer ones, entry and exit order, raised events waiting for the end of a step (`fsm/parallel`) | W3C, "State Chart XML (SCXML): State Machine Notation for Control Abstraction", Recommendation, 2015: sections 3.1.3, 3.13, 4.2 and Appendix D (entry and exit order) |
| Shallow and deep history, recorded on exit, default before the first visit (`history`, `initial`) | Harel 1987, as above, for H and H*; SCXML sections 3.1.4, 3.10 and `exitStates` in Appendix D. History as a property of the state rather than a target of its own is our own |
| Delayed transitions (`after`) | Harel 1987, as above, for timeouts on a state; SCXML section 6.2 for delay and Appendix D for the end of a step. Splitting a step at the moment a delay ends is the event-scheduling of discrete event simulation, as in any textbook on it; asking a guard again each update is our own |
| Machines as data (`fsm/document`) | The JSON format is our own, in the shape of `@margoengine/animator/graph`. Functions by name in place of code follows SCXML's split of a chart from its data model, in spirit only: the format shares nothing with SCXML |
| Snapshots and trace (`fsm/trace`) | The configuration of a statechart, with its history values, as all a machine needs to go on: Harel 1987 and SCXML Appendix D. The snapshot format, restoring with no hooks and the rolling record are our own |

## Save and load: `@margoengine/save`

Written clean room from the specifications below. No source code of serializers, save systems or engines (Unity
prefabs and serialization, Godot scenes, Unreal SaveGame, three.js ObjectLoader, Babylon serializers, localForage,
idb and the like) was read.

| Area | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Overrides | RFC 7386, "JSON Merge Patch" (objects merge, null removes, other values replace) |
| Document format | ECMA-404 / RFC 8259 JSON; our own entity, reference (`{"$ref": id}`) and prefab layout |
| Migration fuzz (batch 167) | No rival package. Seeded version-1 documents are migrated, encoded and decoded by the public API. The generator is Margo's own (`packages/save/scripts/fuzz-roundtrip.mjs`). |
| Compression | RFC 1952 gzip, through the WHATWG Compression Streams standard (`CompressionStream`, `DecompressionStream`) |
| Web Storage adapter | The HTML Standard, Web Storage (`getItem`, `setItem`, `removeItem`, `key`, `length`); RFC 4648 base64 |
| IndexedDB adapter | W3C Indexed Database API 3.0 (open and upgradeneeded, object stores, readonly and readwrite transactions, requests) |
| File adapter | Node.js `fs/promises` documentation (`writeFile` with `signal` and `flush`, `rename` replacing the target) |
| Cloud saves (`./remote`) | RFC 9110, "HTTP Semantics": sections 8.8.1, 8.8.3, 10.2.3, 13.1 to 13.1.2, 13.2 and 15.5.13 (ETags and their comparison, `Retry-After`, `If-Match`, `If-None-Match`, 412). WHATWG Fetch Standard, section 3.3 (the CORS protocol), through a summarising fetch. D. B. Terry et al., "Managing Update Conflicts in Bayou, a Weakly Connected Replicated Storage System", SOSP 1995 (a merge procedure per write). Apache's behaviour was learned by running it, never from its source. |
| Safe writes (`./safe`, and the fixes to `fileStorage` and `indexedDBStorage`) | T. S. Pillai et al., "All File Systems Are Not Created Equal: On the Complexity of Crafting Crash-Consistent Applications", OSDI 2014 (what a crash can leave of a write; a file's fsync does not cover its name). P. Koopman, "32-Bit Cyclic Redundancy Codes for Internet Applications", DSN 2002 (Hamming distances of the 802.3 polynomial). W3C Indexed Database API 3.0, section 2.7 and `IDBTransactionOptions` (`durability`). The frame is this repository's own layout. The tests are our own, in the manner of the paper's crash states; the paper's ALICE tool and its source were not read or used. |
| Save slots (`./slots`) | No outside source: records and rotation are this repository's own layout over the storage above. The thumbnail uses the HTML Standard's `createImageBitmap` (`resizeWidth`, `resizeHeight`), `OffscreenCanvas` and `convertToBlob`; the type of raw bytes is read from their first bytes as the PNG (W3C), JPEG (ITU T.81) and WebP (RFC 9649) formats define them. |
| Engine scene binding (`./engine`) | No outside source: it writes this repository's own objects into the document above. Node poses are compared with the glTF 2.0 specification's `translation`, `rotation`, `scale` and `matrix` of a node. |

## E57 reader and writer in `@margoengine/points/e57`

Written clean room from the standard below. No source code of E57 libraries (libE57, libE57Format, PDAL, pye57,
CloudCompare and the like) was read.

| Area | Source |
| --- | --- |
| File header, pages, XML section, data3D scans, prototype fields, compressed vectors, bitPack bytestreams | ASTM E2807-11, "Standard Specification for 3D Imaging Data Exchange, Version 1.0" |
| Page checksum | CRC-32C: G. Castagnoli, S. Bräuer and M. Herrmann, "Optimization of cyclic redundancy-check codes with 24 and 32 parity bits", IEEE Trans. Communications 41(6), 1993 (reflected polynomial 0x82F63B78) |
| XML subset | W3C XML 1.0 (elements, attributes, character references, CDATA, comments) |
| Pose rotation | Rotation matrix of a unit quaternion (Hamilton's quaternion algebra) |

## Audio mixing and effects: `@margoengine/mixer`

Written clean room from the specifications below. No source code of audio engines, mixers or effect libraries (Tone.js,
Howler, Web Audio API implementations in browsers, FMOD, Wwise, JUCE and the like) was read.

| Area | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Web Audio graph, parameters and timing | W3C Web Audio API specification (AudioContext and OfflineAudioContext, AudioParam automation, GainNode, DelayNode, ConvolverNode, PannerNode, AnalyserNode, DynamicsCompressorNode) |
| Biquad filters | The BiquadFilterNode section of the Web Audio API specification, which states the public RBJ "Audio EQ Cookbook" formulas |
| Compressor curve | Giannoulis, Massberg and Reiss, "Digital Dynamic Range Compressor Design: A Tutorial and Analysis", JAES 60(6), 2012 (static soft-knee curve) |
| Reverb impulse | Exponentially decaying white noise at an RT60 (the definition of reverberation time); mulberry32 pseudo-random generator (public domain) |
| Loop delay | The Web Audio API specification's rules for cycles in the graph (a loop needs a DelayNode and costs one render quantum of 128 frames) |
| Mute, solo and sends | Common mixing-desk behaviour (solo-in-place, solo-safe returns, pre and post fader sends); our own tree rule in `audibleBuses` |

## WebGL2 compatibility layer: `@margoengine/webgl2-compat`

Written clean room from the specifications below. No source code of shader translators or WebGPU-on-WebGL layers
(Tint, Naga, SPIRV-Cross, ANGLE's translator, Dawn, wgpu, three.js and the like) was read.

| Area | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Capability probe and tiers | Khronos WebGL 2.0 specification (`getExtension`, `getParameter`, `getShaderPrecisionFormat`) and the WebGL extension registry entries named in the package; OpenGL ES 3.0 and GLSL ES 3.00 specifications (precision qualifiers) |
| WGSL corpus recorder | W3C WebGPU specification (`GPUDevice` methods, `getCompilationInfo`) |
| WGSL front end (`/wgsl`: lexer, parser, type checker, constant folding, IR) | W3C WGSL specification (textual structure and template-list discovery, grammar, types and memory layout, expressions, statements and behaviors, built-in functions, entry-point interfaces); IEEE 754 for f32 and f16 rounding; mulberry32 (public domain) in the fuzz tests |
| GLSL back end (`/glsl`: WGSL IR to GLSL ES 3.00, helpers for integer division, remainder, shifts, conversions, bit and pack built-ins, uniform reads in WGSL layout, combined samplers) | W3C WGSL specification (expressions, statements, built-in functions and their defined results, memory layout, entry-point interfaces); Khronos GLSL ES 3.00 specification (types, qualifiers, std140 layout, built-in functions, texture lookups, precision) |
| Device (`/device`: GPU, adapter, device, queue, buffers, textures, samplers, bind groups, render pipelines, command encoding and replay, canvas context, install) | W3C WebGPU specification (object model, limits and features, texture formats, render pass load and store, error scopes, device loss, canvas configuration); Khronos WebGL 2.0 and OpenGL ES 3.0 specifications (buffers, textures, samplers, framebuffers, blit, draw calls, sync objects, pixel transfers); WebGL extension registry (EXT_clip_control, WEBGL_provoking_vertex, OES_draw_buffers_indexed, KHR_parallel_shader_compile, EXT_texture_filter_anisotropic, EXT_depth_clamp, EXT_color_buffer_float) |
| CPU tier NaN encoding (one quiet NaN, 0x7fc00000 and f16 0x7e00, in stores, bitcasts, packing and storage textures) | W3C WGSL specification 15.7.1 and 15.7.2 (a runtime NaN or infinity under the finite math assumption is an indeterminate value) and 8.2 (an indeterminate value may be a NaN); ECMA-262 25.1.3.17 NumericToRawBytes (a NaN written to an ArrayBuffer may take any implementation-chosen encoding); IEEE 754-2019 (quiet NaN encoding) |
| Uniform buffers larger than a GL uniform block (read through a data texture) | W3C WebGPU specification, limits table: `maxUniformBufferBindingSize` defaults to 65,536 bytes; OpenGL ES 3.0 specification: `MAX_UNIFORM_BLOCK_SIZE` may be as low as 16,384 (ANGLE's Metal back end reports that) |

## Weather states and hail: `@margoengine/weather`

Written clean room from the specifications and papers below. No source code of weather systems or particle libraries
(Unreal weather and Niagara systems, Unity weather assets, three.js and Babylon particle systems, game weather plugins
and the like) was read. The code of `@margoengine/sky/weather` in this repository was the starting layout for the hail pass.

| Area | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Wind noise | Smooth value noise: lattice values from the PCG output hash (O Neill, "PCG: A Family of Simple Fast Space-Efficient Statistically Good Algorithms for Random Number Generation", 2014) blended with the quintic fade of Perlin, "Improving Noise", SIGGRAPH 2002; our own gust, direction and turbulence layout |
| Random walk | A weighted Markov chain over named presets; mulberry32 pseudo-random generator (public domain) |
| Blending | Smoothstep (the OpenGL and WGSL `smoothstep` definition); shortest-arc angle interpolation |
| Hail path | Constant fall speed (terminal velocity under drag, a design choice) and two ballistic bounces: `y = v t - g t^2 / 2` with a coefficient of restitution on the vertical speed |
| Wet and snowy surfaces | Our own design: roughness and albedo scales for wet surfaces (wet surfaces look darker and shinier) and a slope threshold for snow |
| Ground wetness and snow cover | First-order exponential approach to a target, with separate rise and fall time constants |

## Biome maps: `@margoengine/biomes`

Written clean room from the papers and books below (design spec: item 27.4). No source code of other engines, terrain
or biome tools (Unreal PCG and its Biome Core plugin, Unity terrain and its store assets, Godot add-ons, World Creator,
Gaea, World Machine, Houdini and the like) was read; facts about them come only from their public documentation.

| Area | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Fixed-point library | Exact 64-bit products from 16-bit limbs and integer division with a correction step (textbook integer arithmetic); binary logarithm by repeated squaring of the mantissa (the textbook digit-by-digit method) |
| Trigonometry | Volder, "The CORDIC trigonometric computing technique", IRE Transactions on Electronic Computers, 1959 (vectoring and rotation modes, gain constant) |
| Hashes | Jarzynski and Olano, "Hash functions for GPU rendering", Journal of Computer Graphics Techniques, 2020 (PCG output hash, nested hashing for several inputs) |
| Value noise | Lattice values from those hashes, blended with the quintic fade of Perlin, "Improving Noise", SIGGRAPH 2002; fractal sums and ridged noise in our own integer layout |
| Multi-noise classifier (`/multinoise`) | Our own design: nearest prototype by weighted squared Euclidean distance to a point or box (textbook nearest-prototype rule) on axes normalised to Q16, blended with the Wendland kernel above; the technique name is generic and no other tool's source or data was read |
| Blend kernel | Wendland, "Piecewise polynomial, positive definite and compactly supported radial functions of minimal degree", Advances in Computational Mathematics, 1995 (C2 kernel `(1 - r)^4 (4r + 1)`) |
| Procedural relief and R16 | Our own design: fractal lowlands, ridged mountains under a mask, a smoothstep coast shelf, river valleys carved around polylines |
| Height interpolation | Keys, "Cubic convolution interpolation for digital image processing", IEEE Transactions on Acoustics, Speech and Signal Processing, 1981 (Catmull-Rom weights, a = -0.5) in Q16; linear extension past the edges |
| Arc cosine | Hastings' polynomial, Abramowitz and Stegun, "Handbook of Mathematical Functions", 1964, formula 4.4.45 and 4.4.46 |
| Slope, aspect, curvature, position | Horn, "Hill shading and the reflectance map", Proceedings of the IEEE, 1981 (3x3 gradient); Zevenbergen and Thorne, "Quantitative analysis of land surface topography", Earth Surface Processes and Landforms, 1987 (general, profile and plan curvature); Guisan, Weiss and Weiss 1999 (topographic position index) |
| Depression filling | Barnes, Lehman and Mulla, "Priority-flood: an optimal depression-filling and watershed-labeling algorithm for digital elevation models", Computers and Geosciences, 2014 (min-heap of outlets, ties by index) |
| Distance to water | Meijster, Roerdink and Hesselink, "A general algorithm for computing distance transforms in linear time", 2000 (exact squared Euclidean distance, two separable passes) |
| Wind exposure | Winstral, Elder and Davis, "Spatial snow modeling of wind-redistributed snow using terrain-based parameters", Journal of Hydrometeorology, 2002 (Sx, largest upwind slope angle within 300 m) |
| Orographic rain | Roe, "Orographic precipitation", Annual Review of Earth and Planetary Sciences, 2005 and Roe and Baker 2006 (upslope condensation, downwind drift of the condensate, evaporation on descent); Smith and Barstad, Journal of the Atmospheric Sciences, 2004 (the linear theory this approximates); our own integer march along the wind, lee floor and recovery fetch |
| Temperature | Minder, Mote and Lundquist, Journal of Geophysical Research, 2010 (surface lapse rates below 6.5 K/km, steeper by day than by night); the mean annual latitude profile is our own fit, `|sin lat|^3`, to observed zonal means as plotted in Hartmann, "Global Physical Climatology", 2016; heat load folded about the north-east to south-west axis after McCune and Keon, Journal of Vegetation Science, 2002 |
| Seasons and growing season | Seasonal swing of about 12 sin(latitude) K over the sea growing with continentality (Gorczynski and Conrad continentality indices); degree days of a sinusoidal year, the standard closed-form integral; Korner and Paulsen, Journal of Biogeography, 2004, and Korner, "Alpine Treelines", 2012 (6.4 C growing-season mean, at least 94 days above 0.9 C) |
| Season states (`/seasons`) | McMaster and Wilhelm, "Growing degree-days: one equation, two interpretations", Agricultural and Forest Meteorology, 1997 (degree days as the integral above a base), here clipped to part of the sinusoidal year in closed form; Hock, "Temperature index melt modelling in mountain areas", Journal of Hydrology, 2003 (snow melt from thaw degree days, 3 mm per C day chosen inside its 2.5 to 6 range); green-up full at 200 C day, senescence as the share of cooling between the warmest day and the autumn crossing, and the colour curve are our own design |
| Cold-air pools | Our own engineering form (labelled as such): night cooling `coldAir (1 - exp(-d / 30 m))` with d the Priority-Flood fill depth plus half the depth below the local mean, reduced on wind-exposed ground |
| Evapotranspiration and moisture index | Allen, Pereira, Raes and Smith, FAO Irrigation and Drainage Paper 56, 1998, chapter 3 (Hargreaves, extraterrestrial radiation from latitude and day); Fu's form of the Budyko curve with w = 2.6 (Fu 1981; Zhang et al. 2004) for AET / PET |
| Soil depth | Heimsath, Dietrich, Nishiizumi and Finkel, Nature, 1997 (exponential soil production) and Pelletier and Rasmussen, Water Resources Research, 2009 (depth from the log of convexity, deep in hollows); convexity measured on the 49 m position box, not per 1 m cell |
| Biome classification | Wendland 1995 kernels of the signed distance to each envelope bound (our blend-band layout); ecotone warp by integer value noise and a 3x3 box blur (our design); nearest-envelope fallback in normalised distance (the nearest-interval idea of public block-world generation write-ups, written fresh); largest-remainder rounding to 255 |
| Whittaker classes | Whittaker, "Communities and Ecosystems", 2nd edition, 1975 (the temperature and rainfall diagram); the rectangles in `WHITTAKER` are our own approximation of its regions |
| Holdridge life zones | Holdridge, "Determination of world plant formations from simple climatic data", Science, 1947, and "Life Zone Ecology", 1967: belts at biotemperatures 1.5, 3, 6, 12 and 24 C, rainfall doubling from 62.5 mm, PET ratio 58.93 x biotemperature / rainfall; nearest hexagon centre in the log lattice (our computational form) |
| Preset limits | Plant functional type screening by coldest month, GDD5 and a drought index after Prentice et al., Journal of Biogeography, 1992 (BIOME1) and Kaplan et al., Journal of Geophysical Research, 2003 (BIOME4); the per-preset numbers are our own, rounded from those background values |
| Koppen labels | Beck et al., "Present and future Koppen-Geiger climate classification maps at 1-km resolution", Scientific Data, 2018, after Peel, Finlayson and McMahon 2007 (the 30-class rules); months from our sinusoidal year and rain-season regimes |
| Terrain view and ground weights | Our own design: ground layer rules as Wendland bands like the envelopes, biome weights shared among ground layers and rounded to 255 by largest remainder, generated material textures from value noise with box-filtered mips; drawn through the terrain package's optional weights textures |
| Editor | Our own design: integer brush dabs (quintic, linear or hard falloff) on user rasters, edits kept as plain data for exact replay, undo and redo by raster snapshots; the dirty margin rule (rounds x 2 x the largest radius plus the cluster spread) from our research notes of 2026-10-28 |
| Placement candidates and thinning | Lane and Prusinkiewicz, "Generating spatial distributions for multilevel models of plant communities", Graphics Interface, 2002 (layers placed in size order, each thinned by suitability and the layers above); thinning against each candidate's own hashed number, our layout |
| Clusters | Thomas, "A generalization of Poisson's binomial limit for use in ecology", Biometrika, 1949 (Gaussian offspring); Matern, "Spatial Variation", 1960 (discs); Clark, Silman, Kern, Macklin and HilleRisLambers, "Seed dispersal near and far", Ecology, 1999 (the 2Dt kernel); the shot-noise Cox form and its equality with Neyman-Scott processes of Poisson offspring as in Illian, Penttinen, Stoyan and Stoyan, "Statistical Analysis and Modelling of Spatial Point Patterns", Wiley, 2008 |
| Hard core | Luby, "A simple parallel algorithm for the maximal independent set problem", SIAM Journal on Computing, 1986; Blelloch, Fineman and Shun, "Greedy sequential maximal independent set and matching are parallel on average", SPAA, 2012 (rounds with fixed priorities settle to the greedy set); evaluated lazily per candidate and round, our design |
| Sizes and crowns | de Liocourt, 1898 (reverse-J diameters); Bailey and Dell, Forest Science, 1973 (Weibull); Richards, Journal of Experimental Botany, 1959 (Chapman-Richards height); West, Brown and Enquist, Nature, 1999 (crowns as a power of stem size, exponent 0.75 here); the room rule after Reineke, Journal of Agricultural Research, 1933 (stand density from space filling) with the random sequential adsorption jamming cover 0.547 (Feder, Journal of Theoretical Biology, 1980) |
| Canopy layers | Purves, Lichstein, Strigul and Pacala, "Predicting and understanding forest dynamics using a simple tractable model", PNAS, 2008, and Strigul et al., Ecological Monographs, 2008 (the perfect plasticity approximation); the size-asymmetric light share after Weiner, "Asymmetric competition in plant populations", Trends in Ecology and Evolution, 1990; the 60 % closure is the lower bound of a closed tree canopy (crowns interlocking, generally 60 to 100 % crown cover; open canopy 25 to 60 %) in the Federal Geographic Data Committee's Vegetation Classification Standard, FGDC-STD-005, June 1997 (glossary), and matches the 0.6 to 0.65 cover our densest stands reach under the hard core and room rule; the mortality curve is our calibration |
| Workers and streaming | Our own design (B7): worker replicas made from the same options and changed by the same calls in order (so their integer output is the page's), per-worker priority queues that never reorder across a change, tile affinity by 4 x 4 blocks, prefetch along the camera's smoothed velocity, a per-frame copy budget; stored records in pages of 64 from a free list. Web Workers, transferable ArrayBuffers and AbortSignal as the HTML and DOM standards describe them |
| Edges | Murcia, "Edge effects in fragmented forests", Trends in Ecology and Evolution, 1995; Harper et al., "Edge influence on forest structure and composition in fragmented landscapes", Conservation Biology, 2005 (denser, taller shrubs and smaller trees at edges); the factors are ours |
| Point-pattern statistics | Diggle, "Statistical Analysis of Spatial and Spatio-Temporal Point Patterns", 3rd edition, CRC, 2013 and Illian et al. 2008 (K, L, g, G, F, J and edge corrections); Myllymaki, Mrkvicka, Grabarnik, Seijo and Hahn, "Global envelope tests for spatial processes", JRSS B, 2017; Marsaglia and Marsaglia, "Evaluating the Anderson-Darling distribution", Journal of Statistical Software, 2004; Koenker and Bassett, "Regression quantiles", Econometrica, 1978 |
| Erosion (`/erosion`) | Musgrave, Kolb and Mace, "The synthesis and rendering of eroded fractal terrains", SIGGRAPH 1989 (thermal erosion by an angle of repose); Howard, "A detachment-limited model of drainage basin evolution", Water Resources Research, 1994, and Whipple and Tucker, Journal of Geophysical Research, 1999 (stream power incision); Davy and Lague, "Fluvial erosion/transport equation of landscape evolution models revisited", Journal of Geophysical Research, 2009 (transport capacity, erosion toward it and deposition of the excess); drainage in Priority-Flood order after Barnes, Lehman and Mulla 2014. Our own integer layout: Q16 rates, fixed rounds, an in-place thermal pass in alternating order, lakes filled with sediment, cuts never below the downstream cell. No erosion code of any tool or engine (Gaea, World Machine, World Creator, mapgen4 and the like) was read |
| Roads and paths (`/splines`) | The uniform cubic Catmull-Rom and B-spline bases as in Catmull and Rom, "A class of local interpolating splines", in Barnhill and Riesenfeld (eds.), "Computer Aided Geometric Design", Academic Press, 1974, and textbook spline references (polynomial coefficients of the four control points, evaluated by Horner's rule); point-to-segment distance by projection onto the segment (textbook geometry). Our own integer layout: a fixed number of samples per span, Q16 parameters with floors, a bucket grid of segment reach boxes, beds averaged over the line, weights combined by largest weight then lowest bed. No road or spline tool code (Unreal, Unity, Gaea, World Machine, World Creator and the like) was read |
| Self-thinning bands | Long et al., 2024, and Weller, Ecological Monographs, 1987 (published size-density frontier slopes of about -1.4 to -2.3 by species and site; no universal -3/2) |

## Ragdolls: `@margoengine/ragdoll`

Written clean room from the public descriptions below. No source code of ragdoll systems or engines (Unity, Unreal,
Godot, PhysX, Havok, Jolt, Bullet, three.js, Babylon and the like) was read. Rapier is the physics library underneath for now;
ragdoll reaches it only through the public `@margoengine/physics` API.

| Area | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Swing and twist limits | The swing-twist decomposition of a rotation about an axis (standard quaternion algebra: the twist is the rotation's projection onto the axis, the swing the remainder) |
| Limit impulses | E. Catto, "Iterative Dynamics with Temporal Coherence", GDC 2005 (velocity constraints solved with impulses through the bodies' inverse inertia), applied as an inelastic stop |
| Limit projection | T. Jakobsen, "Advanced Character Physics", GDC 2001 (projecting a violated constraint back by moving the body), here a turn of the child about its joint |
| Capsule inertia | Solid cylinder moments of inertia (standard mechanics tables) |
| Bodies from bones and blending back | Our own design: one capsule per bone in the bone's world frame, joints at the bone origins, kinematic hit boxes that follow the animation, and a per-bone slerp from the frozen physics pose to the animated pose |
| Powered ragdolls (`ragdoll/powered`) | Rapier's published joint motor API; J. Tan, K. Liu, G. Turk, "Stable Proportional-Derivative Controllers", IEEE CG&A 2011, for the implicit spring on the root bone; feeding a target's velocity to a PD controller is textbook control. Raising the mass and inertia of small bodies in a chain is our own measure on real rigs |
| Getting up (`ragdoll/getup`) | Our own design from the public description of the technique (choose a clip by how the body lies, align the character to the body, blend from the ragdoll's pose); turn extraction is the swing-twist decomposition above |
| Body shapes from the skin (`ragdoll/hulls`) | Reuses `rigging/hulls`, which is quickhull as already cited; the box is a plain search over turns about the bone, of our own design |
| Saved profiles (`ragdoll/profile`) | Our own format. Mass shares from P. de Leva, J. Biomechanics 1996; segment lengths from Drillis and Contini, 1966; joint ranges from common published ranges of motion |
| Balance (`ragdoll/balance`) | Capture point from J. Pratt et al., Humanoids 2006; foot placement in the manner of SIMBICON, K. Yin, K. Loken, M. van de Panne, SIGGRAPH 2007; leg angles by the law of cosines; joint torques by the Jacobian transpose from Virtual Model Control, J. Pratt et al., 1997; torques held as forces through the solver's passes from the scheme of M. Macklin et al., "Small Steps in Physics Simulation", SCA 2019. Read in full for the standing and push work: SIMBICON; Small Steps; J. Pratt, J. Carff, S. Drakunov, A. Goswami, "Capture Point: A Step toward Humanoid Push Recovery", Humanoids 2006; B. Stephens, C. Atkeson, "Dynamic Balance Force Control for Compliant Humanoid Robots", IROS 2010. Read in part only: Virtual Model Control; Stable Proportional-Derivative Controllers; S. Kajita et al., "Biped Walking Pattern Generation by using Preview Control of Zero-Moment Point", ICRA 2003; B. Stephens, "Push Recovery Control for Force-Controlled Humanoid Robots", thesis. SIMBICON's page links code: it was not opened |
| Kinematics swept collision (`kinematics-constraints/swept`) | Conservative advancement from B. Mirtich, PhD thesis, UC Berkeley, 1996; per-link motion bound from F. Schwarzer, M. Saha, J.-C. Latombe, WAFR 2002; quaternion from a matrix from K. Shoemake, SIGGRAPH 1985. No other library's source was read |
| Kinematics concave obstacles (`kinematics-constraints/sdf`) | Lipschitz pruning from J. C. Hart, The Visual Computer 12(10), 1996; distances from the repository's own `bvh` and GJK. No other engine's source was read |
| Custom ease curves (`tween/custom-ease`) | Monotone cubic from F. N. Fritsch and R. E. Carlson, SIAM J. Numer. Anal. 17(2), 1980; stroke thinning from U. Ramer, 1972, and D. Douglas and T. Peucker, 1973; the JSON shape is Margo's own. |
| Springs by duration and bounce (`tween` `springOptions`) | The damped oscillator from any mechanics text; `duration` and `bounce` as Apple's public SwiftUI documentation describes them. |
| Linear tween timing (batch 167) | tween.js 25.0.0 is driven as a black box from its user guide (https://github.com/tweenjs/tween.js/blob/main/docs/user_guide.md): 10,000 linear tweens, one 16 ms step. The library source was not opened. Default easing is linear, locked by `packages/tween/test/linear-oracle.test.js`. |

## 2D sprites: `@margoengine/sprites`

Written from the WebGPU and WGSL specifications only (instanced triangle strips, `textureLoad`, straight-alpha blending).
No other engine's sprite code was read. Snapping rounds an image's corner after the camera, so half-pixel pivots still
cover whole pixels; the tests compare every frame byte for byte with an image composed on the CPU in the test itself.

## Pixel camera and layer compositor: `@margoengine/pixel`

Written from the WebGPU and WGSL specifications only (`textureLoad`, `@builtin(position)`, blend factors, dynamic uniform
offsets) and plain rotation maths. No other engine's pixel-art code was read. Each output pixel turns back into the
layer and floors to one source pixel; the tests compare every frame byte for byte with an image composed on the CPU in
the test itself, with quarter turns done by moving pixels forward rather than by the shader's formula.

## Rendering without a page: `@margoengine/headless`

Written from the WebGPU specification (GPUCanvasContext, the 256-byte `copyTextureToBuffer` row alignment, the flag values
of `GPUBufferUsage` and its siblings) and the W3C PNG specification. No other project's headless code or binding source
was read. The real-GPU test compares the bytes with a screenshot of the same scene on a page canvas.

## bzip2 and xz: `@margoengine/decoders/bzip2`, `/xz`

Written from Igor Pavlov's LZMA specification (2015), Lasse Collin and Igor Pavlov's *The .xz File Format* version 1.2.1,
Joe Tsai's bzip2 format notes, and Burrows and Wheeler, *A Block-sorting Lossless Data Compression Algorithm* (DEC SRC
Research Report 124, 1994). No source of libbzip2, liblzma, XZ Utils, the LZMA SDK or any other decoder was read. The two
real libraries ran only as black boxes, through Python's `bz2` and `lzma` modules, to write the test files in
`packages/decoders/tests/fixtures/archives`; VTK and pynrrd ran the same way to write the loader fixtures.

## Particle features: `@margoengine/vfx`

Written clean room from the papers and books below. No source code of particle systems (Unreal Niagara and Cascade,
Unity VFX Graph and Shuriken, Godot particles, three.js and Babylon particle systems, PopcornFX and the like) was read.

| Feature | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Ribbons and trails (`vfx/ribbons`) | W. T. Reeves, "Particle Systems: A Technique for Modeling a Class of Fuzzy Objects", SIGGRAPH 1983 (particles drawn as streaks along their motion); T. Akenine-Moller, E. Haines, N. Hoffman et al., *Real-Time Rendering*, 4th ed., 2018, axial billboards (a strip turned about its own axis toward the eye by a cross product). The ring of records per slot, the birth-angle life stamp and the binary search for a life's records are Margo's own |
| Mesh particles (`vfx/mesh`) | W. T. Reeves, "Particle Systems", SIGGRAPH 1983 (particles as rendered primitives with orientation); O. Rodrigues, "Des lois geometriques qui regissent les deplacements d'un systeme solide", J. Math. Pures Appl. 5, 1840 (rotation about an axis); G. Marsaglia, "Choosing a Point from the Surface of a Sphere", Ann. Math. Stat. 43, 1972, and Archimedes' hat-box result (uniform z and longitude give a uniform axis); J. H. Lambert, *Photometria*, 1760 (diffuse shading); W3C WebGPU specification, `drawIndirect` and instancing. Reusing the billboards' culled list and copying its count into the draw's arguments are Margo's own |
| Sub-emitters and GPU events (`vfx/events`) | W. T. Reeves, "Particle Systems", SIGGRAPH 1983 (particles that spawn further particle systems, the fireworks of *Star Trek II*'s Genesis sequence); G. E. Blelloch, "Prefix Sums and Their Applications", CMU-CS-90-190, 1990, and M. Harris, S. Sengupta, J. D. Owens, "Parallel Prefix Sum (Scan) with CUDA", *GPU Gems 3*, ch. 39, 2007 (stream compaction of the event list by an exclusive scan). The frame-parity halves of the event data, the free-slot tickets past the emitter's budget and the generation flag are Margo's own |
| Lit particles (`vfx/lit`) | S. Green, "Real-Time Approximations to Subsurface Scattering", *GPU Gems*, ch. 16, 2004 (wrap lighting, `max(0, (n.l + w) / (1 + w))`); J. H. Lambert, *Photometria*, 1760 (diffuse shading); the glTF 2.0 specification, Appendix B (the BRDF of the clustered light loop, reused from `@margoengine/clustered`); W3C WebGPU specification, bind groups and `maxBindGroups`. The ball normal from the billboard's disc and spin, and moving the lights to group 2 under an order-independent pass, are Margo's own |
| Depth buffer collision (`vfx/depth-collide`) | W. T. Reeves, "Particle Systems", SIGGRAPH 1983 (particles bounced off surfaces); M. McGuire, M. Mara, "Efficient GPU Screen-Space Ray Tracing", JCGT 3(4), 2014 (testing points against the depth buffer with a thickness); surface reconstruction from depth by unprojecting a texel and its neighbours (Akenine-Moller et al., *Real-Time Rendering*, 4th ed., 2018, screen-space techniques). The reflection with restitution is standard rigid-body impulse. Choosing the neighbour nearer in depth on each axis, and pairing last frame's depth with last frame's camera, are Margo's own |
| Velocity-stretched billboards (`vfx/stretch`) | W. T. Reeves, "Particle Systems", SIGGRAPH 1983 (particles drawn as streaks along their motion over the frame, the motion-blurred sparks of *Star Trek II*); W. T. Reeves, R. Blau, "Approximate and Probabilistic Algorithms for Shading and Rendering Structured Particle Systems", SIGGRAPH 1985; screen-space axis from the velocity's components along the camera's right and up vectors (Akenine-Moller et al., *Real-Time Rendering*, 4th ed., 2018, ch. 13 on billboards). Written from these descriptions; no other engine's code read. |
| Force fields (`vfx/fields`) | H. C. Plummer, "On the problem of distribution in globular star clusters", MNRAS 71, 1911 (the softened potential whose force is m d / (\|d\|^2 + a^2)^1.5); K. Sims, "Particle Animation and Rendering Using Data Parallel Computation", SIGGRAPH 1990 (particles driven by force fields and vector fields); trilinear interpolation of a grid (Press et al., *Numerical Recipes*, 3rd ed., 2007, section 3.6). Written from these descriptions; no other engine's code read. |
| Skinned mesh emitters (`vfx/skinned`) | R. Osada, T. Funkhouser, B. Chazelle, D. Dobkin, "Shape Distributions", ACM TOG 21(4), 2002 (area-weighted triangle choice and a uniform point of a triangle); W. T. Reeves, "Particle Systems", SIGGRAPH 1983 (emission from object surfaces); the glTF 2.0 specification, skins (the posed vertices are the deformer's output, read here as data). Choosing by bind-pose area while placing on the pose is Margo's own. Written from these descriptions; no other engine's code read. |
| Flipbook frame blending (`vfx/flipbook`) | W. T. Reeves, "Particle Systems: A Technique for Modeling a Class of Fuzzy Objects", SIGGRAPH 1983 (a particle's appearance changes over its life); linear interpolation of the current cell and the next, `mix(sample(i), sample(i + 1), fraction)` (WGSL `mix`); W3C WebGPU specification, `textureSample` and `@interpolate(flat)`. The half-texel inset is the existing texture feature. Written from these descriptions; no other engine's source was read. |
| Particle shadows and lights (`vfx/shadows`) | W. T. Reeves, "Particle Systems: A Technique for Modeling a Class of Fuzzy Objects", SIGGRAPH 1983 (particles drawn as their own primitives); T. Akenine-Moller, E. Haines, N. Hoffman et al., *Real-Time Rendering*, 4th ed., 2018, billboard impostors; the front of a sphere under an orthographic projection is centerZ minus the square root of r squared minus x squared minus y squared (elementary geometry); G. E. Blelloch, "Prefix Sums and Their Applications", CMU-CS-90-190, 1990, and M. Harris, S. Sengupta, J. D. Owens, "Parallel Prefix Sum (Scan) with CUDA", *GPU Gems 3*, ch. 39, 2007; B. Karis, "Real Shading in Unreal Engine 4", 2013 (decay 2 selects the windowed inverse-square term); W3C WebGPU specification, depth-only passes and storage writes visible to later commands in one command buffer. Choosing the first live slots in slot order is Margo's own. Written from these descriptions; no other engine's source was read. |
| Particle update nodes (`vfx/nodes`) | W. T. Reeves, "Particle Systems: A Technique for Modeling a Class of Fuzzy Objects", SIGGRAPH 1983 (acceleration times the step updates velocity, then velocity times the step updates position); J. B. Dennis, "First Version of a Data Flow Procedure Language", LNCS 19, 1974 (operators linked by data dependence); A. B. Kahn, "Topological sorting of large networks", CACM 5(11), 1962 (an operator runs after the ones it reads). The operator set, the binding 24 uniform and the live-node filter are Margo's own. Written from these descriptions; no other engine's source was read. |

## External video materials (batch 112)

`@margoengine/video/material` is written from the W3C [WebGPU external texture specification](https://www.w3.org/TR/webgpu/#external-texture), especially the import lifetime, sRGB output encoding, sampling and binding-slot accounting, and [WGSL textureSampleBaseClampToEdge](https://www.w3.org/TR/WGSL/#texturesamplebaseclamptoedge). These specifications are drafts. The sRGB inverse transfer function is the IEC 61966-2-1 function also given by [CSS Color 4](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-color-4/#color-conversion-code). The material integration uses Margo's existing MaterialFeature surface and binding hooks. No other renderer or library source was read or reused. Generated colour VideoFrames and canvas MediaStreams are test stand-ins for decoded movie content; the browser's real external-texture implementation is exercised.

## Page canvas recording (batch 112)

Written from the W3C [MediaStream Recording working draft](https://www.w3.org/TR/mediastream-recording/) (MediaRecorder construction, type support, final dataavailable before stop and errors) and [Media Capture from DOM Elements working draft](https://www.w3.org/TR/mediacapture-fromelement/) (canvas captureStream, origin cleanliness and frame production). Both are drafts. Track clone/stop ownership follows [Media Capture and Streams](https://www.w3.org/TR/mediacapture-streams/). No other library source was read or reused. Existing exporters/capture and headless/video encode supplied frame sequences; neither provides real-time page canvas recording with audio, so this optional entry owns that path.

## 360 and stereo video (batch 112)

Written from the W3C [WebXR Layers working draft](https://www.w3.org/TR/webxrlayers-1/) (mono/stereo-left-right packing, XREquirectLayer and XRMediaBinding) and [WebGPU external textures](https://www.w3.org/TR/webgpu/#external-texture) (dimensions and clamped external sampling). The sphere UV mapping is derived from Margo's own sphereGeometry coordinates; half-texel bounds follow texel centres. Existing XRLayers already owns XR media equirect spheres, and is reused rather than rebuilt. No other renderer/library source was read or reused. Generated panoramas and a stand-in XR binding are explicitly labelled in the evidence.

## Frame-accurate AVC MP4 (batch 112)

Written from the W3C [WebCodecs working draft](https://www.w3.org/TR/webcodecs/) (decode order, presentation timestamps, flush, frame ownership and close), [AVC registration draft](https://www.w3.org/TR/webcodecs-avc-codec-registration/) (avc1 codec string, AVCDecoderConfigurationRecord and length-prefixed access units), and Apple's [QuickTime File Format, video media and sample tables](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/QuickTime/QTFF/QTFFChap3/qtff3.html) plus [movie and edit atoms](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/QuickTime/QTFF/QTFFChap2/qtff2.html). Box/table parsing and timestamp indexing were written here from those descriptions. Margo's existing headless encoder/muxer generates the GPU stand-in; no external demuxer, engine/library source or copied code. No downloaded assets.

## Video alpha and key (batch 112)

Written from W3C [Compositing and Blending, simple alpha/source-over](https://www.w3.org/TR/compositing-1/#simplealphacompositing), [WGSL smoothstep/distance](https://www.w3.org/TR/WGSL/#smoothstep-builtin), and [WebGPU external textures](https://www.w3.org/TR/webgpu/#external-texture). Stacked image rectangles and half-texel clamping are derived from pixel centres. The RGB-distance key and smoothstep opacity are this package's explicit method, not a claim to production matte recovery. The existing Margo material pipeline owns colour conversion and output blending; its direct canvas path blends encoded sRGB values. No other renderer/keying-library code was read or copied, and no external assets downloaded. Generated masks/colours are stand-ins.

## View cube: `@margoengine/viewcube` (batch 109)

Written from the specifications and textbook maths below. No other engine's or CAD application's view cube or axis
gizmo source was read; Autodesk, Blender and SolidWorks behaviour is described from their public user documentation.

| Feature | Source |
| --- | --- |
| Drag to orbit (`drag`) | Spherical coordinates (azimuth about +Y, polar angle from +Y) as in any analytic geometry text, the same turntable the package already animates with; W3C Pointer Events Level 3 (pointer ids, `setPointerCapture`, the `touch-action` property) |
| Home view (`home`, `setHome`) | The same spherical coordinates for the view angles, with the target and distance moved linearly under the cube's existing smoothstep easing (any analysis text); the home button's behaviour is described from Autodesk's public ViewCube user documentation ("Home" view). The button and the exact landing on the stored target are Margo's own |
| Roll buttons (`roll`) | O. Rodrigues, "Des lois geometriques qui regissent les deplacements d'un systeme solide", J. Math. Pures Appl. 5, 1840 (rotation of a vector about an axis), applied to the camera's up about the view direction; rolling the drag offset back by the same angle is a plain 2D rotation. Autodesk's public ViewCube user documentation describes the roll arrows; the quarter-turn snapping and holding the roll while other controls orbit are Margo's own |
| Themes (`theme`, `setTheme`) | W3C WCAG 2.2, definitions of relative luminance and contrast ratio, success criteria 1.4.3, 1.4.6 and 1.4.11 (the 4.5:1, 7:1 and 3:1 thresholds); W3C CSS Custom Properties Level 1; W3C Media Queries Level 5 (`prefers-color-scheme`, `prefers-contrast`); W3C Compositing and Blending Level 1, simple alpha source-over (the translucent fill over a black or white page). The token set and the colour values are Margo's own |
| Axis triad (`viewcube/axes`) | The rows of the camera's view rotation (as in any computer graphics text, here the same rows the cube already turns by) give each axis's screen position and depth; ordering by depth is the painter's algorithm (M. E. Newell, R. G. Newell, T. L. Sancha, "A solution to the hidden surface problem", ACM 1972); W3C CSS Transforms Level 1 (2D `translate` and `rotate`) and CSS 2 `z-index`. Blender's and Autodesk's public user documentation describe an axis gizmo with clickable axis balls; the subclass hooks, ball sizes and colours are Margo's own |

## Mixer automatic sidechain (batch 111)

Written from the W3C [Web Audio Recommendation, AudioWorklet and channel configuration](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-webaudio-20210617/#AudioWorklet),
using its two-input render callback, module registration and explicit speaker channel rules.
Gain smoothing is the directly derived discrete one-pole recurrence g = target + exp(-1/(rate*time)) * (g-target).
No other engine/library source was read or copied. All test signals are generated stand-ins; no downloaded assets.

## Mixer snapshots (batch 111)

Written from the W3C [Web Audio Recommendation, AudioParam automation](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-webaudio-20210617/#AudioParam).
Snapshots compose Margo's existing bus/effect API; interpolation and schema are written here. No external source or assets.

## Mixer exact dynamics (batch 111)

Written from [ITU-R BS.1770-5 Annex 2](https://www.itu.int/dms_pubrec/itu-r/rec/bs/R-REC-BS.1770-5-202311-I!!PDF-E.pdf),
using only its published numeric interpolation data, and [Giannoulis, Massberg and Reiss, JAES 2012](https://eecs.qmul.ac.uk/~josh/documents/2012/GiannoulisMassbergReiss-dynamicrangecompression-JAES2012.pdf)
for the soft-knee curve and one-pole gain smoothing. The lookahead absolute-sum ceiling is derived
here by the triangle inequality, documented in architecture/mixer-true-peak.md. Web Audio processor
registration/channel rules follow the W3C Recommendation cited above. No library/engine source or
paper-linked code was read or reused. No external assets; generated signals are stand-ins.

## Mixer early reflections (batch 111)

Written from the image-source theory in Allen and Berkley, [Image method for efficiently simulating small-room acoustics, JASA 65(4), 1979, first-page model](https://jontalle.web.engr.illinois.edu/Public/AllenBerkley79.pdf).
Only the paper's theory/description was consulted, not its FORTRAN appendix or any implementation.
Six first-order mirror points, inverse path length, excess propagation delay and two-tap fractional
placement are derived here. The existing Margo tail and Web Audio convolution are reused.
No engine/library source or external assets. Generated impulses are stand-ins.

## 2D soft shadows (batch 55)

`@margoengine/lighting2d/soft` is written from published descriptions of 2D area light penumbra filtering: R. Fernando, "Percentage-Closer Soft Shadows", GPU Gems 2, ch. 8, 2005 (penumbra estimation from light source radius and distance between receiver and occluder), and T. Akenine-Moller and U. Assarsson, "Plausible Soft Shadows using Penumbra Wedges", 2002. Angular extent calculation and radial ray filtering across occluder edge lists are Margo-own clean room implementations derived from first principles geometry; no other engine source or library code was read.

## 2D light textures and cookies (batch 55)

`@margoengine/lighting2d/cookie` is written from published descriptions of projective texture masks: M. Segal, C. Korobkin, R. van Widenfelt, J. Foran, and P. Haeberli, "Fast Shadows and Lighting Effects Using Texture Masks", Computer Graphics (SIGGRAPH '92 Proceedings), vol. 26, no. 2, 1992, pp. 249-252. The 2D coordinate transformation, wrapping modes, blend modes and sampling math are Margo-own clean room implementations derived from first principles; no other engine source or library code was read.

## Mixer loudness (batch 111)

Written from [ITU-R BS.1770-5 Annex 1](https://www.itu.int/dms_pubrec/itu-r/rec/bs/R-REC-BS.1770-5-202311-I!!PDF-E.pdf)
(K-filter numeric tables, channel power weights, 400 ms/75% overlap and -70/-10 gates),
[EBU Tech 3341](https://tech.ebu.ch/docs/tech/tech3341.pdf) (400 ms/3 s rectangular windows,
calibration and synthetic case definitions) and its R128 -23 LUFS target. Other-rate filters
are derived algebraically from the published 48 kHz rational transfer function: for r=rate/48000,
substitute old z^-1 = ((1-r)+(1+r)z^-1)/((1+r)+(1-r)z^-1). No library/engine source or reference
implementation was read or reused. Generated cases are stand-ins; no downloaded recordings/assets.
The existing mixer finite-FIR meter supplies true peak and a documented float32 gain margin.

## 2D light masks and layers (batch 55)

`@margoengine/lighting2d` light masking and layer filtering are written from standard first principles: point-in-polygon ray-crossing test derived from W. Randolph Franklin, "PNPOLY - Point Inclusion in Polygon Test", 1970 (Jordan curve theorem), and bitwise layer masking. The WGSL shader bounds tests and CPU reference functions are Margo-own clean room implementations; no other engine source or library code was read.

## 2D global illumination (batch 55)

`@margoengine/lighting2d/gi` is written from published descriptions of radiance cascades for 2D global illumination: Alexander Sannikov, "Radiance Cascades: A Novel Approach to Calculating Global Illumination", 2023. Ray interval partitioning across cascade levels, angular ray distribution, ray-segment and ray-circle intersections, and irradiance integration are Margo-own clean room implementations derived from first principles; no other engine source or library code was read.

## 2.5D height shadows and self-shadowing (batch 55)

`@margoengine/lighting2d/height` is written from standard 3D perspective and directional shadow projection geometry (similar triangles and ray-slab / ray-cylinder intersections along height intervals) applied to 2D top-down and isometric game projections. Height-interval testing, ground shadow projection, and surface self-shadowing are Margo-own clean room implementations derived from first principles; no other engine source or library code was read.

## Emissive sprites and bloom hookup (batch 55)

`@margoengine/lighting2d/emissive` is written from standard first principles: radial and box distance metrics for sprite boundaries, polynomial distance attenuation for emitted light falloff, luminance thresholding, and bloom HDR intensity scaling. The WGSL shader routines and CPU reference functions are Margo-own clean room implementations derived from first principles; no other engine source or library code was read.

## Audio occlusion (batch 110)

Written from the [W3C Web Audio Recommendation](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-webaudio-20210617/) sections BiquadFilterNode, GainNode and AudioParam automation. The sampled segment geometry and interpolation are Margo-own first-principles design choices. The qualitative attenuation plus low-pass approximation follows the published sound occlusion descriptions in [Funkhouser et al., Survey of Methods for Modeling Sound Propagation in Interactive Virtual Environment Systems, SIGGRAPH 2002 course notes](https://www.cs.princeton.edu/~funk/course02.pdf). No engine or library source was read. Reuses Margo's BVH raycaster. No downloaded assets; generated tones and boxes are stand-ins.


### Batch 168 OIT measurement

Babylon.js 8.33.0 is an external black box under engine-bench/batch-168. Only its public Scene,
WebGPUEngine and material APIs are driven. Renderer source was not opened. The oracle is a clean-room
primary-ray intersection of five emissive coverage panes and exact ordered over composition, from
Porter and Duff, "Compositing Digital Images" (1984), https://doi.org/10.1145/800031.808606.
Weighted-blending interpretation: McGuire and Bavoil (2013), https://jcgt.org/published/0002/02/09/.
The public depth-peeling controls are documented at
https://doc.babylonjs.com/typedoc/classes/babylon.depthpeelingrenderer . No refraction is claimed.


### Batch 168 sunsky measurement

The external Mitsuba 3.9.1 scalar_rgb environment is evaluated only through its public Python API,
`load_dict`, `SurfaceInteraction3f.wi` and emitter `eval`. No library source or coefficient tables were read.
Public model, axes, parameters and units: https://mitsuba.readthedocs.io/en/latest/src/generated/plugins_emitters.html#sun-and-sky-emitter-sunsky .
Hosek and Wilkie (2012), "An Analytic Model for Full Spectral Sky-Dome Radiance", DOI
https://doi.org/10.1145/2185520.2185591, identifies the rival model. The tested Margo shader remains
Preetham, Shirley and Smits (1999), with the already documented Margo tuning. No coefficients are imported.

## Audio Doppler (batch 110)

Implemented from the classical stationary-medium source/observer formula in [OpenStax University Physics Volume 1, 17.7](https://openstax.org/books/university-physics-volume-1/pages/17-7-the-doppler-effect), with current world radial velocities and a documented 0.95 Mach clamp. Buffer playback and automation follow the [W3C Web Audio Recommendation, AudioBufferSourceNode](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-webaudio-20210617/#AudioBufferSourceNode). No engine or library implementation was read. Generated sine tones and motion are stand-ins; no external files.


## Batch 168 staging ray reference

Ray/sphere intersection follows the quadratic sphere description in PBRT 4e section 6.2,
https://pbr-book.org/4ed/Shapes/Spheres . Cosine-weighted hemisphere sampling follows the probability
derivation in appendix A.5.3, https://pbr-book.org/4ed/Sampling_Algorithms/Sampling_Multidimensional_Functions .
The harness was written independently; no renderer implementation files were read or copied.
An independent projected-solid-angle integral, R^2*h/d^3 for a sphere entirely above the ground, validates
the traced visibility. This is uniform environment occlusion, not a fitted directional-light shadow.


## Batch 168 independent OIT path trace

The closing cross-check drives Cycles through the public bpy API only, with a Transparent BSDF and
Emission mixed by coverage, matching the unlit-pane transport. Public node descriptions:
https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/shader_nodes/shader/transparent.html and
https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/shader_nodes/shader/emission.html . Renderer code was
not opened. Camera, meshes, node setup, float readback and comparison are independently authored in
`scripts/rival-oit-cycles.py` and `.mjs`; generated EXR, float data and engine pictures stay in engine-bench.
